Step 0c, 2026-10-01, revised 2026-10-02 after the 0c review: the forward-flight yaw policies compared on the yaw model (yaw_model/policy_compare.py, policies.py; records step0c_runs_20261001.jsonl/.csv)
PX4-Autopilot HEAD 4e1baea97c (clean); 5363 leg records, 0 errors; the model as step 0a left it (forward.py, controller.py: G1 48/48, G2, G3, forward_out_20261001.txt; controller.set_rate_cap gained an optional per-direction cap, off by default)

=== In short (details in the sections below and the findings at the end) ===
(the gate's legs: free surge, except where the labelled speed needs the surge held: the gliders' 300 s holds and every A 0.5 leg, "held")
- HEAD: on 2527 and 2522 a full stick parks the integrator at 0.300 and the release turns on 67..145 deg in 120 s, 191..192 deg in all.
- SCHED with the speed known, 2.5-6 m/s: the turn holds the cap and the release is at most -2.3 deg in all after a 20 s hold (2522 2.5 m/s full 20 s ground_W0), +32 deg after 300 s (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s).
- The plan's guarantee (the integrator within rho at any speed in the interval, k right) holds only where u_lo is past the ramp, w(u_lo) = 1: its interval term budgets against FF0 + k u_lo, the ramp supplies FF0 + w(u_lo) k u_lo. The term written on the FF supplied (SCHED_CB*, an addition) keeps the budget wherever u_hi stays past the ramp, and lowers the integrator where the interval straddles it: on the 38 legs of 2527 and 2522 at 2.5-6 m/s that ended the stick with u_lo inside the ramp, the largest integrator at release goes from +0.188 to +0.149 (none at the limit either way); on the review's own example, 2522 gliding with the 1.05 Pa band (free surge), from 4 m/s (ending the stick at 1.72 m/s): I +0.210 -> +0.126, release +108/+134 -> +65/+80. Where the hull itself slows into the ramp, u_hi follows it in and the cap's blend toward MAX takes over: neither term helps (from 2 m/s (ending the stick at 1.75 m/s): I +0.300 -> +0.300, release +153/+191 -> +159/+191; from 2.5 m/s (ending the stick at 1.71 m/s): I +0.300 -> +0.290, release +154/+191 -> +155/+184). With u_lo at or below AS_SPD_LO (53 legs, ground speed only with the wind from ahead) the two terms are the same and hold their budget, but that budget is rho = min(AS_YR_INT_LIM, A / 2) = AS_YR_INT_LIM on the A 1 plants: the integrator still reaches +0.236 (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail), and the release is bounded only by rho / K_I = 191 deg, the defect's own bound.
- SCHED carries whatever FF it lacks into the release: at 2.5-6 m/s (the gate's legs, the speed the label names), k x0.7 / 1.3 / 2 leave up to -191 deg in all (2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2 held) and the outdoor speed sources up to +144 deg (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail), against HEAD's 191..192 deg after a full stick. The integrator collects an FF error with the time constant (D + P) / K_I, 291 s on 2527 at 6 m/s, so the 300 s holds show 64..91 % of the asymptote at 2.5..6 m/s (the time-constant section; 900 s holds check it).
- SCHED+RESTORE: "in all" is at most +2.5 deg (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x0.7), by construction: it is the put-back over K_I, not the plant. The release's largest excursion in 120 s is the informative number: +2.9 deg on the vectored plants (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x0.7), -64.6 deg on the Cloudship with k from its fins (2512 2.5 m/s full 20 s pitot_bias_sdp). RESTORE alone leaves the stick as HEAD has it. The rule's cost, a yaw trim that changes during the turn, is about that change's I / K_I with or without it (32 deg for 5 % of authority).
- 2 m/s sits on the fins' stall edge, for HEAD as for the schedule: a full stick stalls the fins once r exceeds alpha_stall u / arm (4.18 deg/s at 2 m/s on 2527). HEAD's own margin there is 0.95 x alpha_stall free and 1.02 x with the surge held; the schedule's cap, blended back toward MAX inside the ramp, takes it to 10.0 x. On 2527 a full stick departs (fins past 1.2 x) at entry speeds SCHED up to 2 m/s free, 2.3 held; HEAD up to 1.8 free, 1.9 held (the stall-edge section); where that bites follows from the ramp's ends, 1.5 / 2.5 m/s, which are 0c's choice, not the plan's.
- A 0.5 (pods held to +-90 deg): a pod that cannot point backward delivers the yaw command 1:1 only up to the collective thrust; beyond it at half gain, with as much added surge (at 2527's 4 m/s trim 0.122 a command of 0.25 delivers 0.186 with surge 0.186, the allocator section). On the gliding 2522/A0.5 (trim 0) every unit of yaw adds surge, and a free 300 s turn runs away to 33 m/s. The plan's A = 0.5 is the peak, not a steady authority. Over +-180 deg the forward pod saturates past 1 - T (0.725 at 2527's 6 m/s trim), which A = 1 leaves out too. With the surge held, HEAD has no defect on the A 0.5 twins: its flag stops the integrator.
- At rest: the schedule is bit-identical to HEAD only while the selector's interval at rest stays under AS_SPD_LO (on every plant with ground_W0, perfect, pitot_stuck; pitot_bias_sdp on 10 of 12 legs); ground only outdoors (W 2) puts the cap on at rest (the 48 gate verdicts: 42/48). The restore rule always acts at rest (48 gate verdicts kept: 48/48, traces differ).
- The Cloudship (2512): k from its fins is 16.2 x short of its steady-turn slope. A force balance of the tail's side force against the fins' (no hull side force) gives m |x_t| |x_f| / (x_f - x_t) / authority = 13.8 per rad/s per m/s against the 12.74 measured: the turn's torque is set by the centripetal force and the tail-to-fin gap, not by fin damping. At 4 / 6 m/s, 20 s holds, perfect source: SCHED with k from the fins releases up to +154 deg in all (2512 4 m/s full 20 s); with k the measured slope, up to -5 deg (2512/khold 4 m/s full 20 s).

=== What is compared ===
The plan's structure (d1_universal_architecture_20261001.md 2.가-다) made executable in policies.py and flown through forward.run_leg's per-cycle hook, before the controller of each cycle:
  speed selector: pitot interval from the sample's q = 1/2 rho TAS^2 +- dq (rho 1.225), ground interval [max(u_g - W, 0), max(u_g + W, 0)], intersection when both are usable (empty: the pitot dropped that cycle), a lost source left at once and taken back after 1 s usable, both bounds through a 1 s first-order filter (alpha = dt / (tau + dt)), all lost: u_lo decays to 0, u_hi held while the loop is closed. The filter starts converged (the selector has run since boot; a leg starts in steady flight).
  schedule: FF = FF0 + w(u_lo) k u_lo; r_lim = min(MAX, (A - rho) / (FF0 + k u_hi), rho / (k (u_hi - u_lo))[, c u_lo]); cap = MAX + w(u_hi) (r_lim - MAX), w = interpolate(u, 1.5, 2.5, 0, 1), rho = min(AS_YR_INT_LIM, A / 2); the cap is the stick's full scale and the clamp at the loop input (both); FF0, MAX, INT_LIM the plant's HEAD values (AS_YAWRATE_FF, AS_YAWRATE_MAX, AS_YR_INT_LIM); P and I untouched. The ramp's ends 1.5 / 2.5 m/s are 0c's choice: the plan names the ramp and what it must cover (the sensor floor, about 1.5-2 m/s in the speed document, and the gz fins' stall zone, 2-2.5 m/s), not its values. A is per direction (2.나); every 0c plant has the same A both ways
  policies: HEAD (today); RESTORE (options/D's restore rule, edge zero_or_sign, on HEAD gains: the integrator saved when the stick leaves the deadzone is put back when it returns); SCHED; SCHED_STALL (SCHED with the stall term c u_lo); SCHED+RESTORE; and two additions not in the plan: SCHED+RESTORE_FWD* (SCHED+RESTORE with the put-back done only on a cycle with w(u_lo) > 0, so that it is inert at rest; flown on the primary plants, and at rest on every plant), and SCHED_CB* (after the review: SCHED with the interval term on the FF the ramp supplies, rho / (k (u_hi - w(u_lo) u_lo)); flown on every gated cell SCHED flies)
  speed sources (FF errors fly the perfect source):
    perfect         the true air speed, no selector
    ground_W0       ground W 0
    ground_W2_head  ground W 2 + wind 2 m/s toward 180 deg
    ground_W2_tail  ground W 2 + wind 2 m/s toward 0 deg
    ground_W2_x90   ground W 2 + wind 2 m/s toward 90 deg
    ground_W3_head  ground W 3 + wind 3 m/s toward 180 deg
    ground_W3_tail  ground W 3 + wind 3 m/s toward 0 deg
    ground_W3_x90   ground W 3 + wind 3 m/s toward 90 deg
    pitot_bias_sdp  pitot dq 0.05 Pa, bias +1 Pa + ground W 2 (dq below the zero error, a misconfiguration: dq one SDP3x count, the bias 20x outside it)
    pitot_bias_ms   pitot dq 1.05 Pa, bias +1 Pa + ground W 2 (dq covering the bias: one MS4525DO count, and the band an uncalibrated SDP3x needs for its documented CMODEL 0 offset of 1.05 Pa)
    pitot_dropout   pitot dq 0.05 Pa, lost from the stick onset + ground W 2
    pitot_stuck     pitot dq 0.05 Pa, stuck at its entry value + ground W 2
    the pitot band dq is the plan's tolerance (2.가 허용폭); one count (SDP3x 0.05 Pa, MS4525DO 1.05 Pa, the speed document) is a resolution. A +1 Pa bias reads 1.28 m/s at rest. In the model a pitot is its bias and its band, nothing else: an SDP3x with its band at its documented CMODEL 0 offset (1.05 Pa) is the pitot_bias_ms case exactly (the dqcheck legs below)
  legs (forward.run_leg, HEAD gains per airframe): warm-up 1 s held, Manual 2 s, Acro at t0, 3 s centred, the stick (full 20 s, half 20 s, full 300 s), released 120 s; throttle at trim as the 0b check flies it (gliding at 0 on the drag-free 2522 and 2512); u0 2 / 2.5 / 4 / 6 m/s; noise-free unless the noise section says so. The 300 s hold is 2.4 / 1.5 / 1.0 integrator time constants on 2527 at 2.5 / 4 / 6 m/s, so at the higher speeds it shows only part of a slow FF error's asymptote: the time-constant section gives the asymptotes and checks them with 900 s holds
  the gate's legs: free surge where the hull keeps its label (2527 on its trim thrust; the gliders' 20 s holds); the surge held at the label (run_leg hold_u) on the gliders' 300 s holds, which glide down to the same speed from any entry or tumble, and on every A 0.5 leg, whose yaw adds forward thrust. The free-surge legs there stay as the record, with the speed they flew
  primary plants (all 12 sources, every policy): 2527, 2522. Secondary (A 0.5 twins, 2512, 2517): sources perfect, ground_W0, ground_W2_head, ground_W2_tail, ground_W2_x90, pitot_bias_sdp on the 20 s holds, perfect, ground_W2_head, pitot_bias_sdp on the 300 s hold, no RESTORE_FWD*.
Metrics. FRD, every stick positive: a positive heading change continues the turn, a negative one turns back.
  drift: the heading change over the 120 s release, and "to rest": that plus I(end) / K_I, the rest of the turn the integrator still holds (exact while no flag clips it and it stays inside its limit: the release integrates e = -r, so the remaining heading is I / K_I; checked below). Written 120/rest in deg; "?" where the extrapolation does not hold (a flag in the last 30 s, the integrator at its limit, |r| growing, or a plant that does not settle). With a put-back, "to rest" is the put-back over K_I by construction; the release's excursion is then the plant's number
  stick: r mean over its last 5 s against the setpoint at its end (= the cap at full stick), deg/s; the integrator at release (normalized torque; the defect parks it at AS_YR_INT_LIM 0.3 with no flag)
  reversals: pod swings (vectored; cos(tilt) past +-0.5) or tail flips (Cloudship; outside +-0.02), stick / release; fins: the largest |alpha| / alpha_stall in the stick, S = the share of the stick on the stalled branch
  settle: the time after release from which |r| stays < 0.2 deg/s, in the window; past it, "~" an estimate (quasi-static: r = I / (D + P), I decaying with (D + P) / K_I)

  The Cloudship's steady turn as a force balance (cloudship_fin, rigid: no added mass): the tail's side force F_t at x_t and the fins' F_f at x_f (both from the centre of mass) carry the centripetal m u r, and their moments cancel, so F_t = m u r x_f / (x_f - x_t) and the torque command is |x_t| F_t over the authority: N / (u r) = m |x_t| |x_f| / (x_f - x_t) / authority = 7.315 x 1.902 x 1.553 / 0.349 / 4.478 = 13.84 per rad/s per m/s, against the 12.738 measured (+8.6 %: the balance takes the fins' force as a pure side force and leaves out the bank of the turn). It scales with 1 / (x_f - x_t), the 0.349 m tail-to-fin gap: with the arms taken from the link origin instead, 0.022 m behind the centre of mass, it gives 13.48, -2.6 % for 22 mm. A tail thruster's k has to be measured in a held turn, since a few cm of tail position or fin cp move it a lot

=== The integrator's time constant and the long holds (review finding 9) ===
In a held turn the integrator relaxes toward (D - FF) sp with tau = (D + P) / K_I, D the plant's steady-turn torque per rad/s at the speed (the hold slopes above), P 0.7, K_I 0.09; a hold of T s reaches 1 - e^(-T / tau) of it. Beside it, tau from HEAD's own release decay (full stick 20 s, free surge: -120 / ln(I(120 s) / I at release); the gliders slow down in that release, so theirs is shorter):
| plant | u m/s | D per rad/s | tau s | tau, HEAD release | 300 s in tau | reached in 300 s | in 20 s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2527 | 2 | 8.68 | 104 | 98 | 2.88 | 94 % | 17 % |
| 2527 | 2.5 | 10.75 | 127 | 119 | 2.36 | 91 % | 15 % |

=== The interval term and the ramp (review finding 1): SCHED against SCHED_CB* ===
The plan's interval term rho / (k (u_hi - u_lo)) assumes FF = FF0 + k u_lo; the ramp supplies FF0 + w(u_lo) k u_lo. With k right and the true speed u in the interval, a turn at sp leaves the integrator k (u - w(u_lo) u_lo) sp, at most k (u_hi - w(u_lo) u_lo) sp at u_hi, more than the rho the plan's term allows whenever 0 < w(u_lo) < 1: the plan's promise needs u_lo past the ramp. SCHED_CB* writes the term on the FF supplied, rho / (k (u_hi - w(u_lo) u_lo)): its promise needs only u_hi past the ramp. The two terms are the same wherever w(u_lo) = 1 and wherever w(u_lo) = 0 (u_lo at or below AS_SPD_LO, k (u_hi - 0) either way): there both keep the integrator within rho, which on the A 1 plants is AS_YR_INT_LIM itself (finding 2). Where u_hi is inside the ramp the cap is blended back toward MAX by w(u_hi) and no interval term holds. Every SCHED leg of 2527 and 2522 against the same leg under SCHED_CB* (the gate's legs, every stick, hold, source and k error; not 2512, whose k from the fins parks the integrator whatever the term, nor the A 0.5 twins, where the allocator's knee stops it), by where SCHED's interval sat at the stick's end; "limit" the legs ending the stick with the integrator at AS_YR_INT_LIM, "passes" gate 1 against HEAD (merit or floor, a live stick):

=== What the pods deliver: the 1:1 knee (review finding 8, and the same on +-180 deg) ===
The allocator alone (vectored_actuation.Allocator, the firmware's ActuatorEffectivenessAirship port), a constant yaw command tau against a forward thrust demand T (the trims of 2527 at 2 / 2.5 / 4 / 6 m/s, and 0, the gliders), pods settled: delivered yaw / surge, normalized. Each pod is asked for T -+ tau. A pod at +-90 deg cannot take a negative share, so past tau = T it is clipped at 0 and the other carries T + tau: the yaw is (T + tau) / 2, half gain, with the surge (T + tau) / 2 instead of T. Over +-180 deg the backward pod swings to 180 deg and takes its share, but the forward pod saturates at full thrust past tau = 1 - T: the yaw then grows at half gain while the surge drops. Past either knee the allocator flags the shortfall. (A pod asked for less than its 0.02 engage threshold does not steer: the small-tau cells at small T.)
| tilt range | T | knee | tau 0.1 | tau 0.25 | tau 0.5 | tau 0.75 | tau 1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| +-90 | 0.0000 | 0.0000 | 0.050 / 0.050 F | 0.125 / 0.125 F | 0.250 / 0.250 F | 0.375 / 0.375 F | 0.500 / 0.500 F |
| +-90 | 0.0305 | 0.0305 | 0.065 / 0.065 F | 0.140 / 0.140 F | 0.265 / 0.265 F | 0.390 / 0.390 F | 0.500 / 0.500 F |
| +-90 | 0.0477 | 0.0477 | 0.074 / 0.074 F | 0.149 / 0.149 F | 0.274 / 0.274 F | 0.399 / 0.399 F | 0.500 / 0.500 F |
| +-90 | 0.1221 | 0.1221 | 0.100 / 0.122 | 0.186 / 0.186 F | 0.311 / 0.311 F | 0.436 / 0.436 F | 0.500 / 0.500 F |
| +-90 | 0.2748 | 0.2748 | 0.100 / 0.275 | 0.250 / 0.275 | 0.387 / 0.387 F | 0.500 / 0.500 F | 0.500 / 0.500 F |
| +-180 | 0.0000 | 1.0000 | 0.100 / 0.000 | 0.250 / 0.000 | 0.500 / 0.000 | 0.750 / 0.000 | 1.000 / 0.000 |
| +-180 | 0.0305 | 0.9695 | 0.100 / 0.031 | 0.250 / 0.031 | 0.500 / 0.031 | 0.750 / 0.031 | 0.985 / 0.015 F |
| +-180 | 0.0477 | 0.9523 | 0.100 / 0.048 | 0.250 / 0.048 | 0.500 / 0.048 | 0.750 / 0.048 | 0.976 / 0.024 F |
| +-180 | 0.1221 | 0.8779 | 0.100 / 0.122 | 0.250 / 0.122 | 0.500 / 0.122 | 0.750 / 0.122 | 0.939 / 0.061 F |
| +-180 | 0.2748 | 0.7252 | 0.100 / 0.275 | 0.250 / 0.275 | 0.500 / 0.275 | 0.738 / 0.262 F | 0.863 / 0.137 F |
(F: the yaw shortfall flagged.) So the yaw delivered 1:1 is T with pods that cannot reverse and 1 - T with pods that can, not the A = 0.5 (1 + rho_rev) of the plan's 2.나, which leaves out the collective thrust's share of the pods. With pods that cannot reverse, A = 0.5 is the peak (one pod at full thrust) with surge 0.5, and beyond T a unit of yaw costs half a unit of extra forward thrust; on a glider (T = 0) every unit. On +-180 deg the knee matters at speed: at 2527's 6 m/s trim 0.275 it is 0.725, and a turn at the schedule's cap (A - rho) / FF already asks about A - rho = 0.7 of it with k right, so the integrator has about 0.025 before the flag, not rho (the time-constant section: k x0.7 there is flagged, not wound). Flown, free surge (the record), the speed over the stick:
| plant | HEAD | 2 m/s | 2.5 m/s | 4 m/s | 6 m/s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2527/A0.5 | full 20 s | 2.00 -> 4.00 | 2.50 -> 4.33 | 4.00 -> 5.27 | 6.00 -> 6.49 |
| 2527/A0.5 | full 300 s | 2.00 -> 5.41 | 2.50 -> 5.50 | 4.00 -> 5.90 | 6.00 -> 6.66 |
| 2522/A0.5 | full 20 s | 2.00 -> 3.78 | 2.50 -> 4.28 | 4.00 -> 5.79 | 6.00 -> 7.80 |
| 2522/A0.5 | full 300 s | 2.00 -> 29.15 | 2.50 -> 29.66 | 4.00 -> 31.19 | 6.00 -> 33.22 |
With the surge held (the gate's legs), HEAD's integrator at release is +0.000..+0.105 and its release at most +22.7 deg in 120 s (2527/A0.5 6 m/s half 20 s held); parked where the torque stays under the thrust (nothing flagged): 2527/A0.5 6 m/s half 20 s held I +0.105, +23/+67.

=== Findings ===
(every number is read from the records; "where" names the plant, speed and stick, and the source or k error when not the perfect source at k x1, and "held" for a leg with the surge held. "2.5-6 m/s" is the schedule's band: w(u) = 1 at the entry speed; 2 m/s is inside the ramp. The gate's legs throughout: free surge, held on the gliders' 300 s holds and on the A 0.5 twins)
1. The defect at HEAD, the reference every gate is measured against. On 2527 and 2522 (A 1) a full stick parks the integrator at 0.300..0.300 with no flag at every speed (the 15 deg/s setpoint is out of reach), and after the release the hull turns on by 67..145 deg in 120 s and 191..192 deg in all (I / K_I = 0.3 / 0.09 rad), |r| falling under 0.2 deg/s only after ~203..344 s.
2. SCHED removes the defect at 2.5-6 m/s when the speed is known (the perfect source and the indoor ground source W 0, which agree): the turn holds its cap (r over the stick's last 5 s at 0.95..1.04 of the setpoint), the integrator at release is at most -0.0047 (2522 2.5 m/s full 20 s ground_W0), the release turns at most +2.1 deg in 120 s (2527 2.5 m/s full 20 s) and -2.3 deg in all (2522 2.5 m/s full 20 s ground_W0), and |r| is under 0.2 deg/s within 2.3 s. After a 300 s hold the release is at most +32 deg in all (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s): the FF the schedule gives is 99..106 % of the plants' steady-turn torque (the k section), and the integrator takes the rest over minutes. The price is the turn rate: the cap is what the authority reaches with rho in reserve (full stick, 20 s, 2.5 / 4 / 6 m/s: 2527 cap 3.59 / 2.43 / 1.65 deg/s; 2522 cap 3.70 / 2.50 / 1.69 deg/s), where HEAD turns at 2527 3.59 / 2.44 / 1.67 deg/s; 2522 3.07 / 2.02 / 1.37 deg/s with its integrator parked.
3. The plan's guarantee and the ramp (review finding 1). The schedule promises that with k right the integrator stays within rho at any true speed in the interval. Its interval term rho / (k (u_hi - u_lo)) assumes FF = FF0 + k u_lo, but the ramp supplies FF0 + w(u_lo) k u_lo, and the cap is re-opened toward MAX by w(u_hi), not w(u_lo): the promise holds only with u_lo past the ramp (u_lo >= AS_SPD_HI). SCHED_CB* (an addition) writes the term on the FF supplied, rho / (k (u_hi - w(u_lo) u_lo)), whose promise needs only u_hi past the ramp. Every SCHED leg of 2527 and 2522 against the same leg under SCHED_CB* (the gate's legs, every source and k error), by where SCHED's interval sat at the stick's end: 2.5-6 m/s, u_lo past the ramp: 160 legs (152 identical), at the limit 1 -> 1, the largest integrator -0.300 (2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2 held) -> -0.300 (2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2 held), the largest release in all -191 -> -191 deg, gate-1 passes 160 -> 160; 2.5-6 m/s, u_lo inside the ramp, u_hi past it: 38 legs (11 identical), at the limit 0 -> 0, the largest integrator +0.188 (2527 4 m/s full 300 s pitot_dropout) -> +0.149 (2527 6 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail), the largest release in all +112 -> +95 deg, gate-1 passes 38 -> 38; 2.5-6 m/s, u_lo at or below AS_SPD_LO, u_hi past the ramp: 53 legs (14 identical), at the limit 0 -> 0, the largest integrator +0.226 (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail) -> +0.236 (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail), the largest release in all +144 -> +150 deg, gate-1 passes 42 -> 40; 2.5-6 m/s, u_hi inside the ramp: 19 legs (18 identical), at the limit 1 -> 1, the largest integrator -0.300 (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2) -> -0.300 (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2), the largest release in all -189 -> -189 deg, gate-1 passes 19 -> 19; 2 m/s, u_lo past the ramp: 1 legs (1 identical), at the limit 0 -> 0, the largest integrator -0.018 (2522 2 m/s full 20 s pitot_bias_sdp) -> -0.018 (2522 2 m/s full 20 s pitot_bias_sdp), the largest release in all -10 -> -10 deg, gate-1 passes 1 -> 1; 2 m/s, u_lo inside the ramp, u_hi past it: 17 legs (1 identical), at the limit 0 -> 0, the largest integrator +0.258 (2527 2 m/s full 300 s pitot_bias_ms) -> +0.259 (2522 2 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail held), the largest release in all +164 -> +165 deg, gate-1 passes 17 -> 17; 2 m/s, u_lo at or below AS_SPD_LO, u_hi past the ramp: 22 legs (4 identical), at the limit 1 -> 0, the largest integrator +0.299 (2522 2 m/s full 300 s ground_W3_tail held) -> +0.299 (2527 2 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_head), the largest release in all +191 -> +190 deg, gate-1 passes 20 -> 18; 2 m/s, u_hi inside the ramp: 50 legs (14 identical), at the limit 2 -> 3, the largest integrator -0.300 (2527 2 m/s full 300 s pitot_stuck) -> -0.300 (2527 2 m/s full 300 s pitot_stuck), the largest release in all +190 -> +184 deg, gate-1 passes 43 -> 44. The review's own example, 2522 gliding with the 1.05 Pa band (free surge, the record): from 2 m/s (ending the stick at 1.75 m/s): I +0.300 -> +0.300, release +153/+191 -> +159/+191; from 2.5 m/s (ending the stick at 1.71 m/s): I +0.300 -> +0.290, release +154/+191 -> +155/+184; from 4 m/s (ending the stick at 1.72 m/s): I +0.210 -> +0.126, release +108/+134 -> +65/+80; from 6 m/s (ending the stick at 2.08 m/s): I +0.036 -> +0.031, release +17/+23 -> +14/+19. So the failures split three ways. Where the interval straddles the ramp (u_lo inside it, u_hi past it) they are the formula's inconsistent weights, and the supplied-FF term lowers them; it cannot hold a budget the blend has already given up, so where the hull itself slows into the ramp (the gliders from 2 and 2.5 m/s, and every 2 m/s leg) u_hi follows it in and neither term helps (item 5). Where u_lo is at or below AS_SPD_LO (ground speed only, the wind from ahead) the two terms are the same and keep their budget, rho, which on A 1 is AS_YR_INT_LIM: those failures are finding 2's, not the weights'. The supplied-FF term's price is a tighter cap where u_lo is in the ramp: gate 1 counts 16 dead sticks under SCHED_CB* against 13 under SCHED (2522 2 m/s half 20 s pitot_dropout; 2522 2.5 m/s half 20 s pitot_dropout; 2527 4 m/s full 20 s ground_W3_x90). The source families are in the interval-term section.
4. rho on the A 1 plants (review finding 2). rho = min(AS_YR_INT_LIM 0.3, A / 2 0.5) = 0.3 = AS_YR_INT_LIM on 2527, 2522 and the Cloudships, so "the integrator at or below rho" includes the parked state with no flag, the defect itself, and the schedule alone bounds the release only by rho / K_I = 191 deg, HEAD's bound. The interval term is built to let a wide but correct interval use all of rho. Flown (2527 and 2522, every source and k error): SCHED ends the stick at the limit on 5 of 360 legs, SCHED_CB* on 5 of 360; at nominal k SCHED 2527 2 m/s full 300 s pitot_stuck; SCHED_CB* 2527 2 m/s full 300 s pitot_stuck; SCHED 2522 2 m/s full 300 s ground_W3_tail held; SCHED_CB* 2522 2 m/s full 300 s pitot_stuck held. Only rho a strict fraction of AS_YR_INT_LIM (rho = m min(INT_LIM, A / 2), m < 1) bounds the release below 191 deg, by m x 191 deg; a formula question for the owner, beside the restore rule (For the owner, 3).
5. 2 m/s is the fins' stall edge, for HEAD as for the schedule (review finding 12). A full stick stalls the fins once r passes alpha_stall u / arm, 4.18 deg/s at 2 m/s on these hulls. HEAD (cap 15 deg/s, FF0, its integrator parked) turns 2527 at about 4.2 deg/s there: the fins at 0.95 x alpha_stall from a free 2.0 m/s (the pod-swing kick takes u to 2.07 m/s, item 13) and 1.02 x with the surge held at 2.0; from a free 1.8 m/s 1.81 x; held at 2.0 for 300 s it departs (6.18 x, r 15.2 deg/s). SCHED's cap at a free 2 m/s onset is 9.70 deg/s, w(u_hi) = 0.5 blending it back toward MAX, over the stall rate: on 2527 it departs (fins past 1.2 x) from 1.8, 1.9, 2 m/s free and 1.8, 1.9, 2, 2.1, 2.2, 2.3 m/s held, HEAD from 1.8 m/s free and 1.8, 1.9 m/s held (the stall-edge table; 2522: SCHED 1.8, 1.9 / 1.8, 1.9, 2, 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, HEAD none / none). The boundary is the stall-rate condition r_cap > alpha_stall u / arm, and both policies sit on it: HEAD at 2 m/s within a few per cent of the stall angle, the schedule's blended cap moving the edge up on 2527 by 0.2 m/s free and 0.4 m/s held. The 2 m/s gate-1 failures are that edge, not a HEAD advantage. The speed where the schedule bites follows from the ramp's ends, 1.5 / 2.5 m/s, which are 0c's choice: the plan names the ramp, not its values. The stall term c u_lo sits inside the same blend and lowers the cap by under 1 deg/s (item 6), and FF is short there by (1 - w) k u.
6. The stall term as the plan has it (SCHED_STALL) does not earn its place. With the speed known at 2.5-6 m/s it binds on 0 of 24 full-stick legs (the authority term is lower there); at 2 m/s it sits inside the ramp's blend (item above). And where the selector's lower bound reaches 0 while its upper bound is past the ramp, c u_lo = 0 makes the cap 0: on 46 of the full-stick legs on 2527 and 2522 the cap touched 0, and on 28 the stick was dead, the hull turning under 1 deg in the whole stick (sources and speeds: ground_W2_head at 2.5/4 m/s; ground_W2_x90 at 2 m/s; ground_W3_head at 2.5/4/6 m/s; ground_W3_x90 at 2/2.5 m/s), against 1 such leg(s) for SCHED. The longest, 2522 6 m/s full 300 s ground_W3_head held, turns +0.0 deg in the whole stick against SCHED's +103.0. Gate 1 now counts a dead stick (under 5 % of HEAD's heading change in the stick) as no pass: SCHED_STALL has 81, SCHED 13 (the gate section).
7. Inside the ramp FF is short by (1 - w(u_lo)) k u_lo, and the integrator takes it: a 300 s full stick at 2.5 m/s slows 2527 (free surge, trim thrust) to 2.43 m/s (w 0.93) and leaves +0.047 at release, a release of +21/+32 deg (120 s / in all, perfect source). The gliders' 300 s holds are flown held, at w = 1.
8. k x0.7 (FF short by 30 %), 2527 and 2522 at 2.5-6 m/s: SCHED releases at most +24 deg in all after a 20 s hold (2527 2.5 m/s full 20 s k x0.7) and +102 deg in 120 s, +160 deg in all after 300 s (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x0.7). The integrator collects the FF error times the setpoint with the time constant (D + P) / K_I (1.9-4.8 min here), so the 300 s holds show only part of the asymptote, (D - FF) sp / K_I stopped at the limit or at the allocator's flag: up to +162 deg (2522 6 m/s); where FF is short and the turn needs more than the pods deliver 1:1, the shortfall flag stops the integrator first (2527 4 m/s at +0.174, 2527 6 m/s at +0.017); the 900 s holds (2527 4 / 6 m/s free, 2522 6 m/s held) release +111 / +12 / +156 deg against +111 / +11 / +156 predicted (the time-constant section). SCHED+RESTORE: at most +2 deg in all (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x0.7).
9. k x1.3 (FF over by 30 %), 2527 and 2522 at 2.5-6 m/s: SCHED releases at most -15 deg in all after a 20 s hold (2522 2.5 m/s full 20 s k x1.3) and -71 deg in 120 s, -111 deg in all after 300 s (2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x1.3 held). The integrator collects the FF error times the setpoint with the time constant (D + P) / K_I (1.9-4.8 min here), so the 300 s holds show only part of the asymptote, (D - FF) sp / K_I stopped at the limit or at the allocator's flag: up to -120 deg (2522 2.5 m/s); the 900 s holds (2527 4 / 6 m/s free, 2522 6 m/s held) release -97 / -90 / -112 deg against -97 / -90 / -112 predicted (the time-constant section). The integrator collects the FF error times the setpoint with the time constant (D + P) / K_I (1.9-4.8 min here), so the 300 s holds show only part of the asymptote, (D - FF) sp / K_I stopped at the limit or at the allocator's flag: up to -191 deg (2527 2.5 m/s); the 900 s holds (2527 4 / 6 m/s free, 2522 6 m/s held) release -191 / -192 / -191 deg against -191 / -191 / -191 predicted (the time-constant section). SCHED+RESTORE: at most +2 deg in all (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2).
11. Ground speed only, outdoors (W 2 or 3, a wind of that speed): with the wind from ahead u_g - W reaches 0, FF falls to FF0 and the interval term rho / (k (u_hi - u_lo)) sets the cap near 1 deg/s; FF0 alone carries a few % of the turn's torque, so the stick answers through the integrator, slowly (4 m/s, full stick: 2527 ground_W2_head: r 0.19 of 1.08 deg/s, +3.0 deg in the 20 s stick; 2522 ground_W2_head: r 0.15 of 1.07 deg/s, +2.1 deg in the 20 s stick; HEAD 2527 r 2.44, +38.5 deg; 2522 r 2.02, +29.8 deg). Over a 300 s hold the turn carries the wind round the hull, u_lo swings to 0 and back, and the integrator climbs toward rho: up to +0.226 at release (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail), a release of +92 deg in 120 s (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail) and +144 deg in all (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail); SCHED_CB* on the same legs up to +150 deg in all (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail). SCHED+RESTORE over every wind case at 2.5-6 m/s: at most +1 deg in all (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_tail).
12. The pitot cases, SCHED, 2.5-6 m/s, the largest release in all: pitot_bias_sdp -62 deg (2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s pitot_bias_sdp held); pitot_bias_ms +64 deg (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s pitot_bias_ms); pitot_dropout +108 deg (2522 4 m/s full 300 s pitot_dropout held); pitot_stuck -28 deg (2527 4 m/s full 300 s pitot_stuck). A +1 Pa bias reads high (2.5 m/s as 2.81, 4 m/s as 4.20). pitot_bias_sdp sets dq to one SDP3x count (0.05 Pa), a resolution, 20 x under the bias: the band never contains the truth, still intersects ground W 2, and the selector keeps the biased value (2527 at the end of a 300 s hold from 2.5 / 4 m/s: [2.63, 2.66] / [3.83, 3.85]), so FF over-supplies and the release turns back. That is a band set below the zero error, a misconfiguration, not a property of the sensor class. pitot_bias_ms sets dq 1.05 Pa, which covers the bias, and is also the band an uncalibrated SDP3x needs for its documented CMODEL 0 offset (1.05 Pa; the dqcheck legs are this case field for field): the design input is that row's trade-off, a band that covers the bias against the FF lost to a lower u_lo inside the ramp (2522 at the end of a 300 s hold, held at 2.5 / 4 m/s: [2.48, 3.10] / [3.99, 4.40]). A pitot stuck at its entry speed over-supplies as the turn bleeds the speed (a turn back). A dropout leaves the pitot at once and flies as ground only (W 2), the wind case above without the wind.
13. Pod swings, full stick at 2.5-6 m/s, stick/release (number of legs): 2527: HEAD 1/0 x4, 1/1 x2; RESTORE 1/1 x6; SCHED 1/1 x6; SCHED+RESTORE 1/1 x6 | 2522: HEAD 1/0 x6; RESTORE 1/0 x6; SCHED 1/0 x3, 1/2 x3; SCHED+RESTORE 1/0 x6. A full stick swings one pod into the rear at its onset under every policy (the yaw couple exceeds the collective thrust). The onset has a surge kick (review finding 11): the swinging pod gets no thrust until it passes 90 deg while the other pushes forward with the whole couple, so the hull is pushed forward for the swing's 1.5 s. 2522 HEAD 2 m/s: u 2.00 -> 2.10, Fx up to 4.7 N; 2522 SCHED 2 m/s: u 2.00 -> 2.36, Fx up to 16.4 N; 2522 SCHED 4 m/s: u 4.00 -> 4.27, Fx up to 13.0 N; 2527 HEAD 2 m/s: u 2.00 -> 2.15, Fx up to 8.5 N; 2527 SCHED 2 m/s: u 2.00 -> 2.34, Fx up to 18.0 N; 2527 SCHED 4 m/s: u 4.00 -> 4.20, Fx up to 15.3 N. Once both pods are on, the steady couple adds no surge (Fx +0.00 N over the second after the swing). This kick, not the steady couple, is what lifts 2522 at 2 m/s up the ramp (u 2.39 at the stick's end); it is the allocator's, worth a check in SITL (0b records the pod reversals). After the release the pod swings back forward once the torque falls under the thrust: HEAD's parked integrator keeps the torque up through most of the 120 s (still turning), the others drop it at once; on 2522, which flies at zero thrust, a pod released at zero demand stays where it is. The policies add no swing in forward flight.
14. RESTORE alone puts the integrator back at the release, on every gated plant and speed, with no speed and no k: "in all" at most +2.5 deg (2527 2 m/s full 20 s), which is the put-back over K_I by construction wherever no flag clips the release (it integrates e = -r, so the rest of the turn is the integrator it starts with); the plant shows in the release's excursion, at most -12.8 deg in 120 s (2512 2 m/s full 20 s), and its 120 s heading change at most -8.0 deg (2512 2 m/s full 20 s). It leaves the stick as HEAD has it: 2527's full stick still turns at 3.59 / 2.44 / 1.67 deg/s against 15 (2.5 / 4 / 6 m/s), the integrator still parks at 0.300 with no flag during it, and at the release the rule throws the parked integrator away in one cycle, a jump of up to -0.300 (2527 2 m/s full 20 s) on top of the setpoint's own step.
15. SCHED+RESTORE keeps SCHED's stick and makes the release independent of the speed source and of k. Over every gated plant at 2.5-6 m/s, every stick, source and k error, "in all" is at most +2.5 deg (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x0.7), by construction (the put-back over K_I, no flag clipping the release), against SCHED alone's -191 deg (2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2 held, and -121.8 deg in 120 s, 2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2 held). The informative number is the release's excursion in 120 s: at most +2.9 deg on the vectored plants (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x0.7); the Cloudship (k from its fins, item below) swings back by up to -64.6 deg first (2512 2.5 m/s full 20 s pitot_bias_sdp): the put-back of its parked integrator lets the sideslip it held unwind, and the integrator returns the heading over minutes. The put-back is the integrator SCHED would have released with: up to +0.300 (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2), largest where k or the source was furthest off. At 2 m/s it does not save the stalled turn (an excursion of up to +74 deg, 2527 2 m/s full 20 s ground_W0).
16. The restore rule's cost, which no 0c condition exercises (the plan's wind moves only the ground speed, so the air-relative trim never changes and a restored trim is never stale): a yaw couple of 0.05 of the authority at 4 m/s, full stick (2522's 300 s held), releases 120 s / in all: 2527 dist_const: HEAD 20 s +106/+223, SCHED 20 s +1/+2, SCHED+RESTORE 20 s +1/+1, RESTORE 20 s +1/+1, HEAD 300 s +107/+223, SCHED 300 s +2/+3, SCHED+RESTORE 300 s +1/+1, RESTORE 300 s +1/+1; 2527 dist_gone: HEAD 20 s +90/+191, SCHED 20 s -12/-28, SCHED+RESTORE 20 s -14/-31, RESTORE 20 s -14/-31, HEAD 300 s +91/+191, SCHED 300 s -1/-4, SCHED+RESTORE 300 s -14/-31, RESTORE 300 s -14/-31; 2527 dist_arrives: HEAD 20 s +106/+223, SCHED 20 s +15/+31, SCHED+RESTORE 20 s +16/+33, RESTORE 20 s +16/+33, HEAD 300 s +107/+223, SCHED 300 s +5/+9, SCHED+RESTORE 300 s +16/+33, RESTORE 300 s +16/+33; 2522 dist_const: HEAD 20 s +124/+223, SCHED 20 s -0/-1, SCHED+RESTORE 20 s +1/+1, RESTORE 20 s +1/+1, HEAD 300 s +108/+223, SCHED 300 s -7/-16, SCHED+RESTORE 300 s +1/+1, RESTORE 300 s +1/+1; 2522 dist_gone: HEAD 20 s +104/+191, SCHED 20 s -15/-30, SCHED+RESTORE 20 s -16/-31, RESTORE 20 s -16/-32, HEAD 300 s +93/+191, SCHED 300 s -11/-23, SCHED+RESTORE 300 s -15/-31, RESTORE 300 s -15/-32; 2522 dist_arrives: HEAD 20 s +122/+223, SCHED 20 s +15/+28, SCHED+RESTORE 20 s +18/+32, RESTORE 20 s +18/+32, HEAD 300 s +108/+223, SCHED 300 s -5/-10, SCHED+RESTORE 300 s +18/+32, RESTORE 300 s +17/+32. A couple that changes during the turn costs about its own I / K_I (32 deg for 0.05) with or without the rule, because at speed the integrator learns it over minutes, not seconds; the rule adds back what the integrator learned during the hold (the 300 s rows). A couple that stays is free.
17. At rest (gate 2), bit-identical to HEAD on every plant and both sticks, by policy: SCHED: inert with ground_W0, perfect, pitot_stuck; partly with pitot_bias_sdp (10 of 12; not on 2522/A0.5, 2527/A0.5); not with ground_W2_head, ground_W2_tail, ground_W2_x90, ground_W3_head, ground_W3_tail, ground_W3_x90, pitot_bias_ms, pitot_dropout; SCHED_STALL: inert with ground_W0, perfect, pitot_stuck; partly with pitot_bias_sdp (10 of 12; not on 2522/A0.5, 2527/A0.5); not with ground_W2_head, ground_W2_tail, ground_W2_x90, ground_W3_head, ground_W3_tail, ground_W3_x90, pitot_bias_ms, pitot_dropout; +RESTORE_FWD*: inert with ground_W0, perfect, pitot_stuck; partly with pitot_bias_sdp (10 of 12; not on 2522/A0.5, 2527/A0.5); not with ground_W2_head, ground_W2_tail, ground_W2_x90, ground_W3_head, ground_W3_tail, ground_W3_x90, pitot_bias_ms, pitot_dropout; SCHED+RESTORE: inert with no source; not with ground_W0, ground_W2_head, ground_W2_tail, ground_W2_x90, ground_W3_head, ground_W3_tail, ground_W3_x90, perfect, pitot_bias_ms, pitot_bias_sdp, pitot_dropout, pitot_stuck; RESTORE: identical on 0 of 12 (the rule acts at every release). The schedule is inert at rest only while the selector's interval at rest stays under AS_SPD_LO: ground only with W >= AS_SPD_LO reads [0, W] at rest whatever the air does and switches the cap on (outdoors without a pitot the selector cannot tell calm air from a wind); the 1.05 Pa band reads up to 1.31 m/s at rest; a dropout falls back to ground W 2; and on the A 0.5 twins a full stick pushes the hull forward from rest, so the +1 Pa reading crosses AS_SPD_LO some seconds in. SCHED_CB* is SCHED at rest wherever w(u_hi) = 0. The restore rule acts at every release, at rest too. The 48 at-rest gate verdicts with each policy on (flown again with the revised hook): HEAD - 48/48; SCHED ground_W0 48/48; SCHED_STALL ground_W0 48/48; SCHED+RESTORE ground_W0 48/48; +RESTORE_FWD* ground_W0 48/48; RESTORE - 48/48; SCHED ground_W2_head 42/48; SCHED_STALL ground_W2_head 41/48 (the gate-lines and samples comparison is in the gate section).
18. SCHED+RESTORE_FWD* (the addition: the put-back only on a cycle with w(u_lo) > 0) is bit-identical to HEAD at rest with the inert sources (above) and releases as SCHED+RESTORE on 262 of 360 forward legs. It drops the put-back wherever the selector's lower bound is under AS_SPD_LO at the release, which is not only at rest: the stalled 2 m/s turns, and every ground-only or dropout case whose u_g - W reaches 0 at the release. The legs where it differs, by source (all speeds): ground_W0 1, ground_W2_head 16, ground_W2_tail 4, ground_W2_x90 14, ground_W3_head 20, ground_W3_tail 8, ground_W3_x90 16, k x0.7 1, k x1.3 2, k x2 3, perfect 1, pitot_dropout 12; the largest, 2527 2 m/s full 300 s k x2, releases +186/+184 against SCHED+RESTORE's +20/+17. So it gives up the safety net exactly when the speed source cannot vouch for forward flight.
19. A 0.5 (the pods held to +-90 deg; review finding 8). The allocator delivers the yaw command 1:1 only up to the collective thrust T: past it the pod that would push backward is clipped at 0 and the other carries T + tau, so the yaw is (T + tau) / 2, half gain, with the surge (T + tau) / 2 instead of T (at 2527's 4 m/s trim 0.122 a command of 0.25 delivers 0.186 with surge 0.186; the allocator section). So the steady yaw available without adding surge is T (0 on the gliding 2522), not the A = 0.5 the plan's 2.나 assumes delivered 1:1: an input to A. Flown free, the yaw drives the speed (HEAD full stick 300 s: 2527/A0.5 2 m/s 2.00 -> 5.41; 2527/A0.5 2.5 m/s 2.50 -> 5.50; 2527/A0.5 4 m/s 4.00 -> 5.90; 2527/A0.5 6 m/s 6.00 -> 6.66; 2522/A0.5 2 m/s 2.00 -> 29.15; 2522/A0.5 2.5 m/s 2.50 -> 29.66; 2522/A0.5 4 m/s 4.00 -> 31.19; 2522/A0.5 6 m/s 6.00 -> 33.22), so the gate flies the twins with the surge held. Held, HEAD's integrator stays at +0.000..+0.105 and its release is at most +22.7 deg in 120 s (2527/A0.5 6 m/s half 20 s held): every full stick asks one pod to push backward, the shortfall is flagged and the integrator stopped. Where the torque stays under the thrust nothing is flagged and it parks: 2527/A0.5 6 m/s half 20 s held I +0.105 (flag up on 46 % of the stick), releases +23/+67; SCHED there +0/+0. SCHED lowers the setpoint (rho 0.25, A - rho 0.25: the full-stick cap 2527/A0.5 1.30 / 0.84 / 0.57; 2522/A0.5 1.39 / 0.88 / 0.59 deg/s at 2.5 / 4 / 6 m/s, where HEAD turns at 2527/A0.5 1.28 / 0.94 / 0.80; 2522/A0.5 0.74 / 0.47 / 0.32); its largest release in all +1.6 deg (2527/A0.5 6 m/s full 300 s held) with the speed known, +80 deg (2527/A0.5 4 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_head held) over every source, where a wide interval keeps the torque under the thrust, nothing is flagged, FF is short and the integrator winds; SCHED_CB* +80 deg (2527/A0.5 4 m/s full 300 s ground_W2_head held).
20. The Cloudship (2512). k from its fins (0.7884) is 16.2 x short of what its turns need (the steady-turn slope, 12.738 per rad/s per m/s). A force balance shows why (review finding 16): the tail's side force at x_t -1.902 m and the fins' at x_f -1.553 m (from the centre of mass) carry the centripetal m u r and cancel each other's moment, so the torque command per rad/s per m/s is m |x_t| |x_f| / (x_f - x_t) / authority = 13.84 (m 7.31 kg, authority 4.478 N m), against the 12.74 measured: the turn's torque is set by the centripetal force and the 0.349 m tail-to-fin gap, not by fin damping. It is that sensitive to geometry: with the arms from the link origin (22 mm behind the centre of mass) it gives 13.48, so a tail thruster's k has to be measured in a held turn (the plan's procedure), not computed from the fins. With k from the fins the schedule supplies 6 % of the turn's torque and the integrator parks as at HEAD: 2 m/s r 3.49/15.00 I +0.300 +76/+178; 4 m/s r 1.63/15.00 I +0.243 +54/+154; 6 m/s r 1.05/11.54 I +0.186 +24/+118. With k the measured slope: SCHED 2 m/s r 1.86/15.00 I +0.300 +58/+163; SCHED 4 m/s r 1.09/1.05 I -0.008 -6/-5; SCHED 6 m/s r 0.68/0.67 I -0.004 -3/-2; SCHED+RESTORE 2 m/s r 1.86/15.00 I +0.300 -24/-0; SCHED+RESTORE 4 m/s r 1.09/1.05 I -0.008 -5/-0; SCHED+RESTORE 6 m/s r 0.68/0.67 I -0.004 -2/-0. HEAD: 2 m/s r 1.39 I +0.300 +88/+189; 4 m/s r 0.66 I +0.300 +67/+190; 6 m/s r 0.41 I +0.300 +42/?. The stall term from the fins' geometry never binds on it (c u = 16.7 deg/s at 2 m/s, over MAX). The 300 s holds (review finding 7): flown free, the gliding hull loses its speed or tumbles (HEAD from 4 / 6 m/s ends the stick at u 1.79 / -0.61 m/s; SCHED at 1.86 / 1.95), so the gate flies them held at the label: HEAD +60/+189 / +51/+190 / +35/+190 / +24/+191, SCHED (k from the fins) +56/+186 / +44/+183 / +15/+102 / +15/+131, SCHED with k measured -61/-54 / -8/-4 / -4/-2 / -2/-1, at 2 / 2.5 / 4 / 6 m/s (free: HEAD +67/+81 / +74/+153 / +27/+61 / +222/+187, SCHED +224/+188 / +66/+172 / +83/+186 / +70/+176).
21. 2517 (not gated). None of the policies stabilises it: no policy changes P, so none changes the loop's damping, and the plant stays directionally unstable at speed. SCHED makes the spin worse at 4 m/s: its FF (from the fins, 3.20 against HEAD's FF0 0.05) pushes harder into the departure, r peak 62.9 deg/s against HEAD's 51.6 and 70 % of the stick stalled against 49 %. Full stick, 20 s: HEAD 2 m/s r peak 29.9 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (28 % stalled), released +58 deg in 120 s, r -0.37 deg/s at +120 s; HEAD 4 m/s r peak 51.6 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (49 % stalled), released +95 deg in 120 s, r -1.37 deg/s at +120 s; RESTORE 2 m/s r peak 29.9 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (28 % stalled), released -9 deg in 120 s, r +0.23 deg/s at +120 s; RESTORE 4 m/s r peak 51.6 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (49 % stalled), released +81 deg in 120 s, r -2.95 deg/s at +120 s; SCHED 2 m/s r peak 30.4 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (26 % stalled), released +60 deg in 120 s, r -0.25 deg/s at +120 s; SCHED 4 m/s r peak 62.9 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (70 % stalled), released +69 deg in 120 s, r -3.95 deg/s at +120 s; SCHED_STALL 2 m/s r peak 30.3 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (26 % stalled), released +60 deg in 120 s, r -0.25 deg/s at +120 s; SCHED_STALL 4 m/s r peak 62.9 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (70 % stalled), released +69 deg in 120 s, r -3.95 deg/s at +120 s; SCHED+RESTORE 2 m/s r peak 30.4 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (26 % stalled), released -2 deg in 120 s, r +0.49 deg/s at +120 s; SCHED+RESTORE 4 m/s r peak 62.9 deg/s, fins 5.6 x stall (70 % stalled), released +82 deg in 120 s, r -2.70 deg/s at +120 s. The cap lowers the setpoint, not the departure; the restore rule puts the integrator back on a hull that is still spinning.
22. gz's gyro noise (three seeds, 2527 and 2512 at 4 m/s) moves the 120 s release by at most HEAD 0.04, RESTORE 0.02, SCHED 0.11, SCHED+RESTORE 0.02, SCHED_STALL 0.11, +RESTORE_FWD* 0.02 deg.
23. What wins where (the evidence for the owner's choices; no decision here). (a) Where the schedule's assumptions hold, 2.5-6 m/s with the speed known within its interval and k right, SCHED alone removes the defect and keeps a proportional stick (item 2). (b) Its formula guarantees the integrator only with u_lo past the ramp, and on the A 1 plants only up to rho = AS_YR_INT_LIM, the defect's own 191 deg (items 3, 4); writing the interval term on the FF supplied (SCHED_CB*) extends the guarantee to u_hi past the ramp. (c) SCHED alone carries any FF error (a wrong k, the ramp, a wide or biased interval) into the release, toward its asymptote over several minutes, up to the same 191 deg as HEAD (items 8-12); the restore rule removes that whatever the error ("in all" by construction; the 120 s excursion is the plant's number, items 14-15), at the price of a stale trim when the yaw trim changes during the turn (item 16). That is the input to remaining decision 1. (d) Neither fixes 2 m/s, where HEAD and the schedule both sit on the fins' stall edge and the ramp's blend lifts the cap over it (item 5), nor the Cloudship with k from its fins (k has to come from a held turn). (e) The stall term as the plan has it finds no use and kills the stick where u_lo reaches 0 (item 6). (f) For pods that cannot reverse, A = 0.5 is a peak with surge, not a steady authority (item 19). (g) At rest the schedule is inert only with sources whose interval at rest stays under AS_SPD_LO; the restore rule never is, and gating it on forward flight (the addition) also drops it when the source cannot vouch for the speed (items 17-18).

Gate verdicts, in short (gate 1 on the gate's legs: passes = smaller on merit + within the floor, both with a live stick; dead sticks and undecided passes counted apart):

=== For the owner: formula questions for the plan's remaining decisions (evidence, no decision) ===
  1. The interval term. The plan's rho / (k (u_hi - u_lo)) is consistent with its FF only where w(u_lo) = 1 (u_lo >= AS_SPD_HI). Writing it on the FF supplied, rho / (k (u_hi - w(u_lo) u_lo)), keeps the promise for any u_lo while u_hi is past the ramp (tested on 3000 random intervals), and changes nothing where w(u_lo) is 1 or 0. Flown (SCHED_CB*, the gate's legs of 2527 and 2522 at 2.5-6 m/s): on the 38 legs whose interval ended the stick with u_lo inside the ramp, the largest integrator at release +0.188 -> +0.149 (none at the limit either way); the review's gliding 2522 with the 1.05 Pa band, free surge, from 4 m/s (ending the stick at 1.72 m/s): I +0.210 -> +0.126, release +108/+134 -> +65/+80. It does not help where the hull itself slows into the ramp (item 2). Proposed for the plan's 남은 결정: write the interval term on the FF supplied.
  2. Inside the ramp (u_hi < AS_SPD_HI). The cap is blended back toward MAX by w(u_hi), and any blend toward MAX lifts it over the budget's r_lim, so no interval term and no blend weight keeps the integrator within rho there; blending by w(u_lo) for both (one weight) moves the cap further toward MAX whenever u_lo is in the ramp (the interval-term section computes it). This is where the fins also stall, for HEAD too (2527, a full stick departs at entry speeds SCHED up to 2 m/s free, 2.3 held; HEAD up to 1.8 free, 1.9 held). The question is what the schedule should promise inside the ramp: e.g. a cap that blends toward the stall rate alpha_stall u / arm rather than MAX, or a ramp placed below the stall zone; the ramp's ends 1.5 / 2.5 m/s were 0c's choice.
  3. rho on A 1. rho = min(AS_YR_INT_LIM, A / 2) = AS_YR_INT_LIM wherever A >= 2 AS_YR_INT_LIM (every A 1 plant at the default 0.3): the bound "the integrator within rho" is then the defect state, and the release bound rho / K_I = 191 deg is HEAD's. rho = m min(AS_YR_INT_LIM, A / 2) with m < 1 bounds it at m x 191 deg (m 0.5: 95 deg) at the price of a lower cap wherever the interval term binds (it scales with rho). Beside the restore rule, the other way to bound the drift independently of the values.
  4. A and the collective thrust (2.나). The allocator delivers a yaw command 1:1 only up to a knee set by the thrust T the pods also carry: T for pods that cannot reverse (beyond it half gain with as much added surge: 2527 at 4 m/s, T 0.122, a command of 0.25 delivers 0.186 with surge 0.186), 1 - T for pods that can (beyond it half gain with the surge dropping: 0.725 at 2527's 6 m/s trim, where the schedule's A - rho = 0.7 leaves the integrator about 0.025 before the flag instead of rho). The plan's A = 0.5 (1 + rho_rev) leaves T out: a steady A would be min(0.5, T) or 1 - T at the operating point, 0 on a gliding hull with pods that cannot reverse. The A formula is the input.
  5. A per direction (2.나) is now carried (PlantConfig.A_dir, a per-direction cap through controller.set_rate_cap); every 0c plant has the same A both ways, so no result moves. A tail without its CA_R_REV bit gets A- = 0: its negative stick has no propulsive yaw at speed, and the positive one keeps its cap (tested, test_policies.py).
  6. Remaining decision 1 (a value-independent bound on the release). Without one, an FF error leaves its asymptote I_inf / K_I after a long turn, up to 191 deg (flown at 2.5-6 m/s: -191 deg, 2522 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x2 held; the time-constant section has the asymptotes). The restore rule removes it whatever the error, its release excursion at most +2.9 deg in 120 s on the vectored plants (2527 2.5 m/s full 300 s k x0.7); its cost is a stale trim when the yaw trim changes during the turn, about the change's own I / K_I (the disturbance section). Option 3 (a strict-fraction rho) bounds the drift without touching the integrator at the release.
